| order by Timestamp desc
// SecurityEvent EventID 4663 = object access. AccessMask 0x10 / 0x40 / 0x1010
// indicate read access to lsass.exe — credential-dump signature. Audit
// Object Access > Audit File System policy required.
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where EventID == 4663
| where ObjectName endswith "\lsass.exe"
| where Account !endswith "$"
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account, ObjectName, ProcessName,
          AccessMask, AccessReason
| order by TimeGenerated desc

kubernetes-el Compromised: How a Pwn Request Exploited a Popular Emacs Package

StepSecurity
2026-03-11 6 use cases 1 technique 5 kill-chain phases detected

On March 5, 2026, a threat actor exploited a classic "Pwn Request" vulnerability in the CI workflow of kubernetes-el/kubernetes-el, a popular Emacs package for managing Kubernetes clusters. The attacker stole the repository's GITHUB_TOKEN (with full write permissions), exfiltrated CI/CD secrets, defaced the repository, and injected destructive code.

Actions on Objectives
Detected
Reconnaissance. Attacker researches the target — OSINT, scanning, enumeration.
Weaponization. Coupling exploit code with deliverable payload (e.g. weaponized PDF/Office doc, trojanized installer).
Delivery. Transmitting the weapon (phishing email, USB, watering hole, drive-by, malicious ad).
Exploitation. Triggering the exploit — vulnerability/macro/trust abuse to execute code. → Article-specific behavioural hunt — kubernetes-el Compromised: How a Pwn Request Exploited a Popular Emacs Package
Installation. Establishing persistence on the host — backdoor, service, scheduled task, registry key. → [LLM] Cacheract memdump.py download/execution on CI runner or developer host · File hash IOCs — endpoint file/process match
Command & Control. Beacon to attacker infrastructure for control and tasking. → [LLM] Exfiltration to kubernetes-el attacker webhook.site UUIDs (Pwn Request payload) · Network connections to article IPs / domains
Actions on Objectives. Lateral movement, credential dumping, data theft, ransomware, sabotage. → [LLM] Compromised kubernetes.el destructive payload — Emacs spawning `rm -rf / --no-preserve-root`
Exfiltration to kubernetes-el attacker webhook.site UUIDs (Pwn Request payload) AICommand & Control High
Hunts any process command line or network destination referencing the two webhook.site UUIDs used by the kubernetes-el attacker to exfiltrate the runner's GITHUB_TOKEN and all `isSecret:true` runner-memory values. Either UUID appearing anywhere in the estate is a near-zero-FP indicator that an org CI runner — or a developer endpoint that pulled the poisoned action / Makefile — has executed the funny.sh payload. Rationale: The two webhook.site UUIDs (18c6f9e6… and 2a7334c9…) are explicit IOCs from the article and unique attacker-controlled exfiltration channels — any process / proxy / firewall event referencing either string is essentially a definitive hit. Hex-UUID strings are single-casing so no case enumeration is needed. Cross-checked against: • https://www.stepsecurity.io/blog/kubernetes-el-compromised-how-a-pwn-request-exploited-a-popular-emacs-package • https://programming.dev/post/46990360 • https://github.com/AdnaneKhan/Cacheract

Cacheract memdump.py download/execution on CI runner or developer host AIInstallation High
Hunts for curl/wget pulling AdnaneKhan/Cacheract's memdump.py (the runner-memory secret-scraper used by the kubernetes-el attacker to harvest GITHUB_TOKEN), including the SHA1-pinned URL b0d8565fa1ac52c28899c0cfc880d59943bc04ea used in the final payload. Cacheract is a public offensive CI research tool — any unsanctioned execution in our estate indicates a Pwn-Request-style payload running on a self-hosted runner or developer machine. Rationale: The article pins the payload to a specific Cacheract commit SHA (b0d8565fa1ac52c28899c0cfc880d59943bc04ea) and references both the main-branch URL and the initial gist (nikitastupin/30e525b776c409e03c2d6f328f254965). These strings appear on the CI runner's command line during the curl|sudo python3 invocation — extremely high-fidelity. WebSearch confirmed AdnaneKhan/Cacheract is a real public CI offensive tool that scrapes Runner.Worker process memory for GITHUB_TOKEN. Cross-checked against: • https://www.stepsecurity.io/blog/kubernetes-el-compromised-how-a-pwn-request-exploited-a-popular-emacs-package • https://github.com/AdnaneKhan/Cacheract • https://adnanthekhan.com/2024/12/21/cacheract-the-monster-in-your-build-cache/

  "AdnaneKhan/Cacheract",

Compromised kubernetes.el destructive payload — Emacs spawning `rm -rf / --no-preserve-root` AIActions on Objectives High
Hunts the post-compromise destructive payload: Emacs loading the trojanised kubernetes.el (commit 09e06af) triggers `shell-command-to-string` with `sudo rm -rf / || rm -rf / || sudo rm -rf / --no-preserve-root`. Any host where Emacs (or a shell child of Emacs) attempts the chained rm-rf-with-no-preserve-root pattern is almost certainly an Emacs user who updated kubernetes-el between 04:32 UTC 2026-03-05 and removal from MELPA. Rationale: The destructive payload is a single, distinctive line from the article (`shell-command-to-string "sudo rm -rf / || rm -rf / || sudo rm -rf / --no-preserve-root"`) bound to an Emacs parent process. The combination of Emacs-spawned shell + `rm -rf /` + `--no-preserve-root` is essentially never benign — the no-preserve-root flag exists specifically to defeat rm's safety check on `/`. File-event side catches the trojanised kubernetes.el on disk in .emacs.d / elpa / melpa locations. Cross-checked against: • https://www.stepsecurity.io/blog/kubernetes-el-compromised-how-a-pwn-request-exploited-a-popular-emacs-package • https://programming.dev/post/46990360
ATT&CKT1485 T1059.004 T1195.001
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes Endpoint.Filesystem
union isfuzzy=true
  (DeviceProcessEvents
    | where Timestamp > ago(30d)
    | where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("emacs","emacs-gtk","emacs-nox","emacs.exe","runemacs.exe","emacsclient","emacsclient.exe")
         or InitiatingProcessParentFileName in~ ("emacs","emacs-gtk","emacs-nox","emacs.exe","runemacs.exe")
    | where FileName in~ ("sh","bash","zsh","dash","sudo","rm","cmd.exe","powershell.exe","pwsh.exe")
    | where ProcessCommandLine has "rm" and ProcessCommandLine has "-rf" and ProcessCommandLine has "/"
         and (ProcessCommandLine has "--no-preserve-root" or ProcessCommandLine has "|| rm -rf /" or ProcessCommandLine has "sudo rm -rf /")
    | project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, FileName, ProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessParentFileName),
  (DeviceFileEvents
    | where Timestamp between (datetime(2026-03-05T04:32:00Z) .. ago(0h))
    | where FileName =~ "kubernetes.el"
    | where FolderPath has_any (@"/.emacs.d/",@"/elpa/",@"/melpa/",@"kubernetes-el",@"\.emacs.d\\",@"\elpa\\")
    | project Timestamp, DeviceName, FolderPath, FileName, SHA256, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessAccountName)

         and (SyslogMessage has "--no-preserve-root" or SyslogMessage has "|| rm -rf /" or SyslogMessage has "sudo rm -rf /")
    | project TimeGenerated, Computer, HostIP, ProcessName, ProcessID, SyslogMessage),
  (ImProcessCreate
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
    | where ParentProcessName in~ ("emacs","emacs-gtk","emacs-nox","emacs.exe","runemacs.exe","emacsclient","emacsclient.exe")
    | where TargetProcessName in~ ("sh","bash","zsh","dash","sudo","rm","cmd.exe","powershell.exe")
    | where TargetProcessCommandLine has "rm" and TargetProcessCommandLine has "-rf" and TargetProcessCommandLine has "/"
         and (TargetProcessCommandLine has "--no-preserve-root" or TargetProcessCommandLine has "|| rm -rf /" or TargetProcessCommandLine has "sudo rm -rf /")

File hash IOCs — endpoint file/process match Installation High
Match SHA256/SHA1/MD5 named in the article against EDR file/process telemetry.
ATT&CKT1027
Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem Endpoint.Processes DeviceFileEvents DeviceProcessEvents
// Pre-filter each table BEFORE the union — the engine cannot push
// predicates across `union`, and DeviceFileEvents lacks ProcessCommandLine
// (Initiating only) so column-projection has to differ per side.
union
    ( DeviceFileEvents
        | where Timestamp > ago(7d)
        | where InitiatingProcessAccountName !endswith "$"
        | where SHA256 in~ ("929c6399c4fde4fe236bd6712b2c53f750d9ad3a") or SHA1 in~ ("929c6399c4fde4fe236bd6712b2c53f750d9ad3a") or MD5 in~ ("929c6399c4fde4fe236bd6712b2c53f750d9ad3a")
        | project Timestamp, DeviceName, ActionType, FileName, FolderPath, SHA256,
                  Cmd = InitiatingProcessCommandLine, Source = "DeviceFileEvents" ),
    ( DeviceProcessEvents
        | where Timestamp > ago(7d)
        | where AccountName !endswith "$"
        | where SHA256 in~ ("929c6399c4fde4fe236bd6712b2c53f750d9ad3a") or SHA1 in~ ("929c6399c4fde4fe236bd6712b2c53f750d9ad3a") or MD5 in~ ("929c6399c4fde4fe236bd6712b2c53f750d9ad3a")
        | project Timestamp, DeviceName, ActionType, FileName, FolderPath, SHA256,
                  Cmd = ProcessCommandLine, Source = "DeviceProcessEvents" )
| order by Timestamp desc

// Pre-filter both ASIM tables before the union — same pattern as the
// Defender version so the engine can push hash predicates per-side.
let BadHashes = dynamic(["929c6399c4fde4fe236bd6712b2c53f750d9ad3a"]);
union
    ( ImFileEvent
        | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
        | where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
        | where TargetFileSHA256 in~ (BadHashes)
           or TargetFileSHA1 in~ (BadHashes)
           or TargetFileMD5 in~ (BadHashes)
        | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, EventType,
                  TargetFileName, TargetFilePath, TargetFileSHA256,
                  Cmd = ActingProcessName, Source = "ImFileEvent" ),
    ( ImProcessCreate
        | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
        | where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
        | where SHA256 in~ (BadHashes) or SHA1 in~ (BadHashes) or MD5 in~ (BadHashes)
        | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, EventType = TargetProcessName,
                  TargetFileName = TargetProcessName, TargetFilePath = TargetProcessName,
                  TargetFileSHA256 = SHA256,
                  Cmd = TargetProcessCommandLine, Source = "ImProcessCreate" )
| order by TimeGenerated desc

// Article-specific bespoke detection — kubernetes-el Compromised: How a Pwn Request Exploited a Popular Emacs Package
// Hunts the actual binaries / paths / commandline fragments named
// in the article instead of a generic technique-class template.
DeviceProcessEvents

| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, FileName,
          FolderPath, ProcessCommandLine,
          InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine
| order by Timestamp desc

          FileName, ActionType, InitiatingProcessFileName,
          InitiatingProcessCommandLine
| order by Timestamp desc

// Requires the Microsoft 365 Defender data connector (streams the
// advanced-hunting tables into Sentinel; Timestamp → TimeGenerated).
// Article-specific bespoke detection — kubernetes-el Compromised: How a Pwn Request Exploited a Popular Emacs Package
// Hunts the actual binaries / paths / commandline fragments named
// in the article instead of a generic technique-class template.
DeviceProcessEvents
| where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
| where (FileName in~ ("funny.sh", "memdump.py"))
| project TimeGenerated, DeviceName, AccountName, FileName,
          FolderPath, ProcessCommandLine,
          InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine
| order by TimeGenerated desc

// File-creation events for the named binaries / paths
DeviceFileEvents
| where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
| where ActionType in ("FileCreated","FileModified")
| where (FolderPath has_any ("/tmp/secrets") or FileName in~ ("funny.sh", "memdump.py"))
| project TimeGenerated, DeviceName, AccountName, FolderPath,
          FileName, ActionType, InitiatingProcessFileName,
          InitiatingProcessCommandLine
| order by TimeGenerated desc

// TVM tables are daily snapshots — pin a 1-day window so the engine
// partitions on Timestamp instead of scanning the full retention.
DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities

| order by VulnerabilitySeverityLevel asc, DeviceName asc

// Sentinel has no native TVM table. If MDE→Sentinel streaming is enabled,

DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
| where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)

| order by VulnerabilitySeverityLevel asc, DeviceName asc

Sednit known-bad SHA-1 hash match: Xagent / SlimAgent / BeardShell binaries AIInstallation High
Direct hash hunt for the four SHA-1 samples ESET attributed to Sednit's reactivated implant team (Xagent ancestor, SlimAgent eapphost.dll, BeardShell tcpiphlpsvc.dll, and Xagent reference sample). Highest-fidelity tier — execution of any of these on a managed endpoint is incident-grade. Rationale: All four SHA-1 values are explicitly named in the ESET article (pre-extracted: D0DB619A7A160949528D46D20FC0151BF9775C32 + 99B454262DC26B081600E844371982A49D334E5E) and Table 1 (5603E99151F8803C13D48D83B8A64D071542F01B for eapphost.dll/SlimAgent and 6D39F49AA11CE0574D581F10DB0F9BAE423CE3D5 for tcpiphlpsvc.dll/BeardShell). Hash matches are zero-FP when the file actually executes. Cross-checked against: • https://www.welivesecurity.com/en/eset-research/sednit-reloaded-back-trenches/ • https://attack.mitre.org/groups/G0007/ • https://cert.gov.ua/article/6280829
ATT&CKT1587.001 T1129
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes
Expected false positives · estimated rate: low — model-assessed, tune to your environment
  • Reverse-engineering / malware-analyst workstation deliberately detonating the sample
  • SOC IOC-ingest tool writing the binaries to disk for sandbox replay
  • Threat-intel vendor scanner with the sample bundled in a signature pack
  • Backup recovery of a quarantined sample being restored to original location
Suppression filter
| where DeviceName !in~ ("MALWARE-LAB-01","REVERSE-ENG-VM") and InitiatingProcessFolderPath !has @"\Sandbox\" and InitiatingProcessFolderPath !has @"\Cuckoo\"
Response runbook
1) TRIAGE — Confirm DeviceName / aid is a production host (not malware lab); pull surrounding 10-min process tree for parent (likely svchost.exe or rundll32.exe), child processes spawned, and outbound DNS for icedrive.net / filen.io. 2) CONTAIN — Network-isolate the host via MDE Live Response / Falcon RTR; preserve memory image; do NOT reboot (BeardShell keeps state in memory). 3) HUNT — Sweep org-wide for the other three SHA-1 values, for any svchost or rundll32 with cmdline referencing eapphost.dll or tcpiphlpsvc.dll outside System32, and for HKCU\Software\Classes\CLSID\*\InprocServer32 pointing to user-writable paths.

SlimAgent / BeardShell DLL load with implant filename outside System32 AIInstallation High
Hunts for image-load or rundll32/svchost invocation of eapphost.dll or tcpiphlpsvc.dll from any path other than the legitimate C:\Windows\System32. Both names are Microsoft DLLs that ESET reports Sednit masquerades as in user-writable directories. Rationale: Table 1 of the article names eapphost.dll (SlimAgent) and tcpiphlpsvc.dll (BeardShell). Both are legitimate Microsoft DLL names that exist in System32; loading either from a user-writable path is a high-confidence indicator the masquerade is active. Cross-checked against: • https://www.welivesecurity.com/en/eset-research/sednit-reloaded-back-trenches/ • https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1574/002/ • https://lolbas-project.github.io/lolbas/Binaries/Rundll32/
ATT&CKT1574.002 T1129 T1036.005
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes Endpoint.Filesystem
Expected false positives · estimated rate: low — model-assessed, tune to your environment
  • Sysinternals or Microsoft support tool unpacking a copy of the legitimate DLL to a temp directory for diagnostics
  • Third-party EAP supplicant installer (Cisco AnyConnect, Juniper Pulse) staging eapphost.dll under ProgramData before move
  • Memory-dump analysis tool re-loading the legitimate DLL from a forensic image path
  • Driver/installer self-extracting to %TEMP% before installing to System32
Suppression filter
| where InitiatingProcessFileName !in~ ("setup.exe","msiexec.exe","7z.exe","winrar.exe") and InitiatingProcessParentFileName !in~ ("msiexec.exe","setup.exe")
Response runbook
1) TRIAGE — Compute SHA-1 of the loaded DLL and compare against the four known Sednit hashes; pull the loading process's full command line and parent. 2) CONTAIN — Isolate the host; capture full memory image (BeardShell exfils via Icedrive over HTTPS so disk-only artefacts under-represent the implant). 3) HUNT — Search org-wide for the same filename in user-writable paths over the last 90 days; check HKCU\Software\Classes\CLSID\*\InprocServer32 for entries pointing to the same DLL.
let ImplantNames = dynamic(["eapphost.dll","tcpiphlpsvc.dll"]);
union
(DeviceImageLoadEvents
  | where Timestamp > ago(30d)
  | where FileName in~ (ImplantNames)
  | where FolderPath !startswith @"C:\Windows\System32\" and FolderPath !startswith @"C:\Windows\SysWOW64\" and FolderPath !startswith @"C:\Windows\WinSxS\"
  | project Timestamp, DeviceName, FileName, FolderPath, SHA1, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessAccountName),
(DeviceProcessEvents
  | where Timestamp > ago(30d)
  | where FileName in~ ("rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","svchost.exe")
  | where ProcessCommandLine has_any (ImplantNames)
  | where not(ProcessCommandLine has_any (@"\Windows\System32\", @"\Windows\SysWOW64\"))
  | project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, FileName, ProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessFileName, SHA1)
| order by Timestamp desc

title: SlimAgent BeardShell DLL Filename Masquerade Outside System32
id: 4e6f8a3b-5c7d-49e2-b1a0-3f6c8e9b1d22
status: experimental
description: Detects DLL load of eapphost.dll or tcpiphlpsvc.dll from a path other than %SystemRoot%\System32 / SysWOW64 — Sednit's BeardShell and SlimAgent masquerade as these legitimate Microsoft DLLs.
references:
  - https://www.welivesecurity.com/en/eset-research/sednit-reloaded-back-trenches/
author: SOC Detection Engineering
date: 2026/05/20
tags:
  - attack.t1574.002
  - attack.t1036.005
  - attack.g0007
logsource:
  category: image_load
  product: windows
detection:
  selection:
    ImageLoaded|endswith:
      - '\eapphost.dll'
      - '\tcpiphlpsvc.dll'
  filter_legit:
    ImageLoaded|startswith:
      - 'C:\Windows\System32\'
      - 'C:\Windows\SysWOW64\'
      - 'C:\Windows\WinSxS\'
  condition: selection and not filter_legit
falsepositives:
  - Custom in-house EAP host helper deployed to a non-standard path (rare)
level: high
source:windows.sysmon @EventID:7 (@ImageLoaded:(*\eapphost.dll OR *\EapHost.dll OR *\EAPHOST.DLL OR *\tcpiphlpsvc.dll OR *\TcpIpHlpSvc.dll OR *\TCPIPHLPSVC.DLL)) -@ImageLoaded:(*\Windows\System32\* OR *\Windows\SysWOW64\* OR *\Windows\WinSxS\*)
(#event_simpleName=ProcessRollup2 OR #event_simpleName=ImageHash)
| (regex("(?i)\\\\eapphost\\.dll", field=CommandLine) OR regex("(?i)\\\\tcpiphlpsvc\\.dll", field=CommandLine) OR regex("(?i)\\\\eapphost\\.dll$", field=ImageFileName) OR regex("(?i)\\\\tcpiphlpsvc\\.dll$", field=ImageFileName))
| NOT regex("(?i)\\\\Windows\\\\(System32|SysWOW64|WinSxS)\\\\", field=ImageFileName)
| NOT regex("(?i)\\\\Windows\\\\(System32|SysWOW64|WinSxS)\\\\", field=CommandLine)
| groupBy([aid, ComputerName, ImageFileName, CommandLine], function=[min(@timestamp), count()])
ENDPOINT

BeardShell C2: outbound to Icedrive cloud-storage API as non-browser process AICommand & Control High
Detects egress to icedrive.net / icedrive.com originated by a process that is NOT a known web browser. BeardShell uses Icedrive cloud storage as its C&C channel per ESET; legitimate enterprise use of Icedrive is rare and almost always browser-mediated. Rationale: Article explicitly states BeardShell 'executes PowerShell commands via a legitimate cloud provider used as its C&C channel' and Table 2 documents 'BeardShell relies on Icedrive cloud storage.' Browser exclusion eliminates the only common benign source. Corroborated via Sekoia and CERT-UA reporting. Cross-checked against: • https://www.welivesecurity.com/en/eset-research/sednit-reloaded-back-trenches/ • https://blog.sekoia.io/ • https://cert.gov.ua/article/6280829
ATT&CKT1102.002 T1583.006 T1071.001
Data sourcesNetwork_Traffic.All_Traffic Endpoint.Processes
Expected false positives · estimated rate: low — model-assessed, tune to your environment
  • Power user running the Icedrive native desktop sync client (icedrive.exe)
  • PowerShell automation script intentionally using Icedrive as a backup target
  • Curl/PowerShell health check of Icedrive availability from a sysadmin workstation
  • Security researcher analysing BeardShell in a sandbox VM with internet access
Suppression filter
| where InitiatingProcessFileName !~ "icedrive.exe" and InitiatingProcessCommandLine !has "icedrive-cli"
Response runbook
1) TRIAGE — Identify the InitiatingProcessFileName / ContextImageFileName making the connection; if it is svchost.exe, rundll32.exe, or a generic .NET host, immediately treat as P1. Pull the loaded modules via DeviceImageLoadEvents for tcpiphlpsvc.dll. 2) CONTAIN — Block icedrive.net/icedrive.com at proxy/DNS for the host; network-isolate via MDE / Falcon RTR; capture memory before reboot. 3) HUNT — Pivot on the same SrcIpAddr/aid for filen.io/filen.net (Covenant), and look for COM-hijack persistence on HKCU\Software\Classes\CLSID\*\InprocServer32.

Covenant C2: outbound to Filen cloud-storage API as non-browser process AICommand & Control High
Detects egress to filen.io / filen.net originated by a process that is NOT a known web browser. Sednit's reworked Covenant implant switched to Filen as its C2 transport since July 2025 per ESET; Filen has minimal legitimate enterprise traction. Rationale: Article explicitly attributes Covenant's switch to Filen cloud storage in July 2025 and Table 2 records 'Covenant relies on Filen cloud storage.' Filen has very low enterprise install base, so non-browser egress is high-fidelity. Cross-checked against: • https://www.welivesecurity.com/en/eset-research/sednit-reloaded-back-trenches/ • https://attack.mitre.org/software/S0190/ • https://github.com/cobbr/Covenant

Sednit COM-hijacking persistence via HKCU CLSID InprocServer32 to user-writable DLL AIInstallation High
Detects creation/modification of HKCU\Software\Classes\CLSID\{...}\InprocServer32 default values pointing to a DLL in a user-writable path, with bonus matching on eapphost.dll / tcpiphlpsvc.dll filenames. ESET attributes this exact T1546.015 technique to both BeardShell and SlimAgent. Rationale: Table 2 of the ESET article explicitly maps both implants to T1546.015 COM Hijacking: 'BeardShell and SlimAgent are made persistent by hijacking COM objects.' HKCU\Software\Classes\CLSID\{CLSID}\InprocServer32 is the canonical hijack key; combining with user-writable DLL location or the named implant filenames is high-fidelity. Cross-checked against: • https://www.welivesecurity.com/en/eset-research/sednit-reloaded-back-trenches/ • https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1546/015/ • https://www.bleepingcomputer.com/news/security/
ATT&CKT1546.015
Data sourcesEndpoint.Registry
Expected false positives · estimated rate: medium — model-assessed, tune to your environment
  • Slack/Teams/Spotify/Zoom installing into %AppData%\Local and registering a per-user COM class (legitimate app, user-writable path triggers the heuristic)
  • Visual Studio / .NET SDK developer tools registering COM classes under %AppData%
  • Browser extension installers writing per-user COM hooks
  • Squirrel-based auto-updaters writing self-extracting installs under %LOCALAPPDATA%
Suppression filter
| where not(InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("slack.exe","teams.exe","spotify.exe","zoom.exe","update.exe","squirrel.exe","setup.exe","msiexec.exe")) and not(RegistryValueData has_any (@"\AppData\Local\slack\", @"\AppData\Local\Microsoft\Teams\", @"\AppData\Roaming\Spotify\", @"\AppData\Local\Programs\Microsoft VS Code\"))
Response runbook
1) TRIAGE — Resolve the CLSID via online lookup (Microsoft, ProgID Registry) to identify which COM interface is being hijacked; pull the DLL the value points to and compute its SHA-1 against the four Sednit hashes. 2) CONTAIN — Delete the HKCU\Software\Classes\CLSID\{CLSID}\InprocServer32 override key (the HKLM canonical CLSID is restored automatically); isolate the host; capture memory. 3) HUNT — Search org-wide for other HKCU CLSID overrides pointing to %AppData% / %TEMP% DLLs in the last 90 days; correlate to the host's outbound traffic for icedrive.net and filen.io.
DeviceRegistryEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where ActionType in~ ("RegistryValueSet","RegistryKeyCreated")
| where RegistryKey has @"\Software\Classes\CLSID\" and RegistryKey has "InprocServer32"
| where (RegistryValueName == "" or RegistryValueName =~ "(Default)")
| where RegistryValueData has_any (@"\AppData\", @"\Users\Public\", @"\Windows\Temp\", @"\ProgramData\", "eapphost.dll", "tcpiphlpsvc.dll")
| where not(InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("msiexec.exe","trustedinstaller.exe","regsvr32.exe"))
   or RegistryValueData has_any ("eapphost.dll","tcpiphlpsvc.dll")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, InitiatingProcessAccountName, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine, RegistryKey, RegistryValueName, RegistryValueData
| order by Timestamp desc
ImRegistryEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
| where RegistryKey has @"\Software\Classes\CLSID\" and RegistryKey has "InprocServer32"
| where (RegistryValue == "" or RegistryValue =~ "(Default)")
| where RegistryValueData has_any (@"\AppData\", @"\Users\Public\", @"\Windows\Temp\", @"\ProgramData\", "eapphost.dll", "tcpiphlpsvc.dll")
| where not(ActingProcessName in~ ("msiexec.exe","trustedinstaller.exe"))
   or RegistryValueData has_any ("eapphost.dll","tcpiphlpsvc.dll")
| project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, ActorUsername, ActingProcessName, ActingProcessCommandLine, RegistryKey, RegistryValue, RegistryValueData
| order by TimeGenerated desc
title: Sednit COM Hijacking Persistence HKCU CLSID InprocServer32
id: 6f1a3c84-5d8e-4b92-a0c3-7f9b1d4e2c55
status: experimental
description: BeardShell and SlimAgent persist via COM hijacking. Detects HKCU CLSID InprocServer32 (Default) value pointing to a DLL in a user-writable path or matching known Sednit DLL filenames.
references:
  - https://www.welivesecurity.com/en/eset-research/sednit-reloaded-back-trenches/
  - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1546/015/
author: SOC Detection Engineering
date: 2026/05/20
tags:
  - attack.t1546.015
  - attack.persistence
  - attack.g0007
logsource:
  category: registry_event
  product: windows
detection:
  selection_key:
    TargetObject|contains:
      - '\Software\Classes\CLSID\'
    TargetObject|endswith:
      - '\InprocServer32\(Default)'
      - '\InprocServer32'
  selection_value:
    Details|contains:
      - '\AppData\'
      - '\Users\Public\'
      - '\Windows\Temp\'
      - '\ProgramData\'
      - 'eapphost.dll'
      - 'tcpiphlpsvc.dll'
  condition: selection_key and selection_value
falsepositives:
  - User-scoped legitimate COM registration by an app installed under %AppData% (Slack, Teams, Spotify)
level: high
source:windows.sysmon @EventID:13 (@TargetObject:(*\Software\Classes\CLSID\*\InprocServer32* OR *\software\classes\clsid\*\inprocserver32*)) (@Details:(*\AppData\* OR *\appdata\* OR *\Users\Public\* OR *\Windows\Temp\* OR *\ProgramData\* OR *eapphost.dll* OR *EAPHOST.DLL* OR *tcpiphlpsvc.dll* OR *TCPIPHLPSVC.DLL*))
#event_simpleName=AsepValueUpdate
| regex("(?i)\\\\Software\\\\Classes\\\\CLSID\\\\", field=RegObjectName)
| regex("(?i)InprocServer32", field=RegObjectName)
| (regex("(?i)\\\\AppData\\\\", field=RegStringValue) OR regex("(?i)\\\\Users\\\\Public\\\\", field=RegStringValue) OR regex("(?i)\\\\Windows\\\\Temp\\\\", field=RegStringValue) OR regex("(?i)\\\\ProgramData\\\\", field=RegStringValue) OR regex("(?i)eapphost\\.dll", field=RegStringValue) OR regex("(?i)tcpiphlpsvc\\.dll", field=RegStringValue))
| groupBy([aid, ComputerName, RegObjectName, RegStringValue, ContextImageFileName], function=[min(@timestamp), count()])
ENDPOINT
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime values(Registry.registry_value_data) as registry_value_data values(Registry.user) as user values(Registry.process_name) as process_name from datamodel=Endpoint.Registry where Registry.registry_path IN ("*\\Software\\Classes\\CLSID\\*\\InprocServer32*") AND Registry.registry_hive="HKEY_USERS" AND (Registry.registry_value_data IN ("*\\AppData\\*","*\\Users\\Public\\*","*\\Temp\\*","*\\ProgramData\\*","*eapphost.dll*","*tcpiphlpsvc.dll*")) by host Registry.dest Registry.registry_path Registry.registry_value_name | `drop_dm_object_name(Registry)` | convert ctime(firstTime) ctime(lastTime)
| tstats summariesonly=false count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime values(Registry.registry_value_data) as registry_value_data values(Registry.user) as user values(Registry.process_name) as process_name from datamodel=Endpoint.Registry where Registry.registry_path IN ("*\\Software\\Classes\\CLSID\\*\\InprocServer32*") AND Registry.registry_hive="HKEY_USERS" AND (Registry.registry_value_data IN ("*\\AppData\\*","*\\Users\\Public\\*","*\\Temp\\*","*\\ProgramData\\*","*eapphost.dll*","*tcpiphlpsvc.dll*")) by host Registry.dest Registry.registry_path Registry.registry_value_name | `drop_dm_object_name(Registry)` | convert ctime(firstTime) ctime(lastTime)
SlimAgent keylogger HTML log artefact written to disk (Xagent-lineage colour scheme) AIActions on Objectives Medium
Hunts for HTML / log files written to user-writable paths by a process whose command line references the SlimAgent or BeardShell DLL names, OR by an unsigned host process under svchost/rundll32. Targets the Xagent-derived HTML log format (Figures 5/6 in the ESET article) where keystrokes are encoded with blue/red/green colour tags. Rationale: Article Figures 5 and 6 show SlimAgent emits keylog data as HTML files with the same colour tag definitions inherited from 2015 Xagent source. We can't safely inspect file content at scale, so the proxy signal is HTML/log artefacts written to user-writable staging paths by either (a) a host process loading eapphost.dll / tcpiphlpsvc.dll, or (b) an unexpected non-browser process. Cross-checked against: • https://www.welivesecurity.com/en/eset-research/sednit-reloaded-back-trenches/ • https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1056/001/
ATT&CKT1056.001 T1074.001 T1115
Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem
Expected false positives · estimated rate: medium — model-assessed, tune to your environment
  • Browser writing cached pages or session-restore HTML to %AppData%
  • Office / Outlook saving an HTML draft to %TEMP%
  • Application crash dump generators writing .dat / .log files to %ProgramData%
  • Generic installer writing release-notes HTML to %ProgramData%
Suppression filter
| where not(FolderPath has_any (@"\AppData\Local\Microsoft\Windows\INetCache\", @"\AppData\Local\Microsoft\Edge\", @"\AppData\Local\Google\Chrome\User Data\", @"\AppData\Local\Mozilla\Firefox\", @"\AppData\Roaming\Microsoft\Outlook\"))
Response runbook
1) TRIAGE — Open the file (in an isolated sandbox) and look for `<font color="red">` / `<font color="blue">` / `<font color="green">` tags consistent with the Xagent colour scheme described in Figure 5; compute the file's parent-process command line. 2) CONTAIN — Isolate the host; preserve the file as evidence; capture memory; block egress to icedrive.net + filen.io. 3) HUNT — Look for the named eapphost.dll / tcpiphlpsvc.dll SHA-1 hashes on this host, pivot on the InitiatingProcessId to find sibling files in the same staging directory, and review HKCU\Software\Classes\CLSID\*\InprocServer32 for the persistence anchor.
let ImplantDlls = dynamic(["eapphost.dll","tcpiphlpsvc.dll"]);
let UserWritablePaths = dynamic([@"\AppData\Local\", @"\AppData\Roaming\", @"\Windows\Temp\", @"\Users\Public\", @"\ProgramData\"]);
DeviceFileEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where ActionType in~ ("FileCreated","FileModified")
| where FileName endswith ".html" or FileName endswith ".htm" or FileName endswith ".log" or FileName endswith ".dat"
| where FolderPath has_any (UserWritablePaths)
| where (InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("svchost.exe","rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe") and InitiatingProcessCommandLine has_any (ImplantDlls))
   or (InitiatingProcessFolderPath has_any (UserWritablePaths) and not(InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe","notepad.exe","notepad++.exe","code.exe","outlook.exe","winword.exe","excel.exe","powerpnt.exe","teams.exe","slack.exe","onenote.exe")))
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, InitiatingProcessAccountName, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessFolderPath, InitiatingProcessCommandLine, FileName, FolderPath, SHA1
| order by Timestamp desc
let ImplantDlls = dynamic(["eapphost.dll","tcpiphlpsvc.dll"]);
let UserWritablePaths = dynamic([@"\AppData\Local\", @"\AppData\Roaming\", @"\Windows\Temp\", @"\Users\Public\", @"\ProgramData\"]);
let BenignProcs = dynamic(["chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe","notepad.exe","notepad++.exe","code.exe","outlook.exe","winword.exe","excel.exe","powerpnt.exe","teams.exe","slack.exe","onenote.exe"]);
ImFileEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
| where EventType in~ ("FileCreated","FileModified")
| where TargetFileExtension in~ ("html","htm","log","dat")
| where TargetFilePath has_any (UserWritablePaths)
| where (ActingProcessName in~ ("svchost.exe","rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe") and ActingProcessCommandLine has_any (ImplantDlls))
   or not(ActingProcessName in~ (BenignProcs))
| project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, ActorUsername, ActingProcessName, ActingProcessCommandLine, TargetFilePath, TargetFileName, TargetFileSHA1
| order by TimeGenerated desc
source:windows.sysmon @EventID:11 (@TargetFilename:(*.html OR *.htm OR *.log OR *.dat)) (@TargetFilename:(*\AppData\* OR *\appdata\* OR *\Windows\Temp\* OR *\Users\Public\* OR *\ProgramData\*)) (@CommandLine:(*eapphost.dll* OR *EAPHOST.DLL* OR *tcpiphlpsvc.dll* OR *TCPIPHLPSVC.DLL*) OR @Image:(*\svchost.exe OR *\rundll32.exe OR *\regsvr32.exe))
#event_simpleName=FileWritten
| regex("(?i)\\.(html|htm|log|dat)$", field=TargetFileName)
| regex("(?i)\\\\(AppData|Windows\\\\Temp|Users\\\\Public|ProgramData)\\\\", field=TargetFileName)
| (regex("(?i)\\\\(svchost|rundll32|regsvr32)\\.exe$", field=ContextImageFileName) OR NOT regex("(?i)\\\\(chrome|msedge|firefox|notepad|code|outlook|winword|excel|powerpnt|teams|slack|onenote)\\.exe$", field=ContextImageFileName))
| groupBy([aid, ComputerName, ContextImageFileName, TargetFileName], function=[min(@timestamp), count()])
ENDPOINT
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.

Beaconing — periodic outbound to small set of destinations Command & Control Medium
C2 channel detection via inter-beacon-time stddev / fan-out to single dest.
ATT&CKT1071.001 T1071.004
Data sourcesNetwork_Traffic.All_Traffic DeviceNetworkEvents
DeviceNetworkEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where RemoteIPType == "Public" and ActionType == "ConnectionSuccess"
| project DeviceName, RemoteIP, RemotePort, Timestamp
| sort by DeviceName asc, RemoteIP asc, RemotePort asc, Timestamp asc
| extend prev_dev = prev(DeviceName, 1), prev_ip = prev(RemoteIP, 1),
         prev_port = prev(RemotePort, 1), prev_ts = prev(Timestamp, 1)
| where DeviceName == prev_dev and RemoteIP == prev_ip and RemotePort == prev_port
| extend delta_sec = datetime_diff('second', Timestamp, prev_ts)
| summarize conn_count = count(), avg_delta = avg(delta_sec), stdev_delta = stdev(delta_sec)
    by DeviceName, RemoteIP, RemotePort
| where conn_count > 30 and avg_delta between (30.0 .. 600.0) and stdev_delta < 5.0
| order by conn_count desc
ImNetworkSession
| where TimeGenerated > ago(2h)
| where DvcAction != "Block"
| where ipv4_is_private(DstIpAddr) == false
| summarize ConnCount = count(),
            DistinctMinutes = dcount(bin(TimeGenerated, 1m))
            by DvcHostname, DstIpAddr, DstPortNumber
| where ConnCount > 50 and DistinctMinutes > 30   // sustained, not bursty
| order by ConnCount desc

    from datamodel=Network_Traffic.All_Traffic

    by _time span=10s, All_Traffic.src, All_Traffic.dest
| `drop_dm_object_name(All_Traffic)`
| streamstats current=f last(_time) AS prev_time by src, dest
| eval delta = _time - prev_time
| stats avg(delta) AS avg_delta stdev(delta) AS sd_delta count by src, dest
| where count > 30 AND sd_delta < 5 AND avg_delta>=30 AND avg_delta<=600
| sort - count

Phishing-link click correlated to endpoint execution Delivery High
User received an external email containing a URL, clicked it, and within 60 seconds the recipient's endpoint spawned a scripting interpreter or network downloader (PowerShell / cmd / mshta / curl / certutil / bitsadmin). This is the high-fidelity shape — a raw "user clicked an emailed URL" query is too noisy to alert on because every Microsoft 365 tenant generates thousands of legitimate clicks per day. The exec-correlation step suppresses the noise to roughly the rate at which a click actually drives onto-host execution, which is the actionable signal. Pair with UC_PHISH_LINK_BYPASS (Safe Links override) and UC_PHISH_LINK_NEWDOMAIN for orthogonal high-fidelity variants.
ATT&CKT1566.002 T1204.001 T1059.001
Data sourcesEmail.All_Email Web Endpoint.Processes EmailEvents EmailUrlInfo UrlClickEvents DeviceProcessEvents
// Phishing-link click that drives endpoint execution within 60s.
// Far higher fidelity than "every clicked URL" — most legitimate clicks
// never spawn a non-browser child process, so the join eliminates the
// 99% of noise that makes a raw click query unactionable.
let LookbackDays = 7d;
let SuspectClicks = UrlClickEvents
    | where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
    | where AccountName !endswith "$"
    | where ActionType in ("ClickAllowed","ClickedThrough")
    | join kind=inner (
        EmailEvents
        | where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
        | where DeliveryAction == "Delivered"
        | where EmailDirection == "Inbound"
        | project NetworkMessageId, Subject, SenderFromAddress, SenderFromDomain,
                  RecipientEmailAddress, EmailTimestamp = Timestamp
      ) on NetworkMessageId
    | join kind=leftouter (
        EmailUrlInfo | project NetworkMessageId, Url, UrlDomain
      ) on NetworkMessageId, Url
    | project ClickTime = Timestamp, AccountUpn, IPAddress, Url, UrlDomain,
              Subject, SenderFromAddress, SenderFromDomain, RecipientEmailAddress,
              ActionType;
// Correlate to a non-browser child process spawned within 60 seconds on
// the recipient's device.
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe",
                                         "outlook.exe","brave.exe","arc.exe")
| where FileName in~ ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","cmd.exe","mshta.exe",
                        "rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe",
                        "bitsadmin.exe","certutil.exe","curl.exe","wget.exe")
| join kind=inner SuspectClicks on $left.AccountName == $right.AccountUpn
| where Timestamp between (ClickTime .. ClickTime + 60s)
| project ClickTime, ProcessTime = Timestamp,
          DelaySec = datetime_diff('second', Timestamp, ClickTime),
          DeviceName, AccountName, RecipientEmailAddress, SenderFromAddress,
          Subject, Url, UrlDomain, ActionType,
          FileName, ProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessFileName
| order by ClickTime desc
// UrlClickEvents has no clean Sentinel-native equivalent. The detection
// pivots to: Office Send→Receive proximity to SecurityEvent 4688 of a
// non-browser child process. Lower fidelity than the Defender version.
let LookbackDays = 7d;
let SuspectMailUsers = OfficeActivity
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(LookbackDays)
    | where OfficeWorkload == "Exchange"
    | where Operation =~ "Receive"
    | summarize Subject = any(Subject), Sender = any(Sender)
                by MailboxOwnerUPN, MessageId, EmailTime = bin(TimeGenerated, 1m);
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(LookbackDays)
| where EventID == 4688
| where ParentProcessName has_any ("\chrome.exe","\msedge.exe",
                                     "\firefox.exe","\outlook.exe","\brave.exe")
| where NewProcessName has_any ("\powershell.exe","\pwsh.exe","\cmd.exe",
                                  "\mshta.exe","\rundll32.exe","\regsvr32.exe",
                                  "\wscript.exe","\cscript.exe","\bitsadmin.exe",
                                  "\certutil.exe","\curl.exe","\wget.exe")
| join kind=inner SuspectMailUsers on $left.Account == $right.MailboxOwnerUPN
| where TimeGenerated between (EmailTime .. EmailTime + 60s)
| project TimeGenerated, EmailTime, Computer, Account, ParentProcessName,
          NewProcessName, CommandLine, Sender, Subject
| order by TimeGenerated desc
WEB
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
``` Phishing-link click that drives endpoint execution within 60s ```
| tstats `summariesonly` earliest(_time) AS click_time
    from datamodel=Web
    where Web.action="allowed"
    by Web.src, Web.user, Web.dest, Web.url
| `drop_dm_object_name(Web)`
| rename user AS recipient, dest AS clicked_domain, url AS clicked_url
| join type=inner recipient
    [| tstats `summariesonly` count
         from datamodel=Email.All_Email
         where All_Email.action="delivered" AND All_Email.url!="-"
         by All_Email.recipient, All_Email.src_user, All_Email.url, All_Email.subject
     | `drop_dm_object_name(All_Email)`
     | rex field=url "https?://(?<email_domain>[^/]+)"
     | rename recipient AS recipient]
| join type=inner src
    [| tstats `summariesonly` earliest(_time) AS exec_time
         values(Processes.process) AS exec_cmd, values(Processes.process_name) AS exec_proc
         from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
         where Processes.parent_process_name IN ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe",
                                                   "outlook.exe","brave.exe","arc.exe")
           AND Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","cmd.exe","mshta.exe",
                                            "rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","wscript.exe",
                                            "cscript.exe","bitsadmin.exe","certutil.exe",
                                            "curl.exe","wget.exe")
         by Processes.dest, Processes.user
     | `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
     | rename dest AS src]
| eval delta_sec = exec_time - click_time
| where delta_sec >= 0 AND delta_sec <= 60
| table click_time, exec_time, delta_sec, recipient, src, src_user, subject,
        clicked_domain, clicked_url, exec_proc, exec_cmd
| sort - click_time
``` Phishing-link click that drives endpoint execution within 60s ```
| tstats summariesonly=false earliest(_time) AS click_time
    from datamodel=Web
    where Web.action="allowed"
    by Web.src, Web.user, Web.dest, Web.url
| `drop_dm_object_name(Web)`
| rename user AS recipient, dest AS clicked_domain, url AS clicked_url
| join type=inner recipient
    [| tstats summariesonly=false count
         from datamodel=Email.All_Email
         where All_Email.action="delivered" AND All_Email.url!="-"
         by All_Email.recipient, All_Email.src_user, All_Email.url, All_Email.subject
     | `drop_dm_object_name(All_Email)`
     | rex field=url "https?://(?<email_domain>[^/]+)"
     | rename recipient AS recipient]
| join type=inner src
    [| tstats summariesonly=false earliest(_time) AS exec_time
         values(Processes.process) AS exec_cmd, values(Processes.process_name) AS exec_proc
         from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
         where Processes.parent_process_name IN ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe",
                                                   "outlook.exe","brave.exe","arc.exe")
           AND Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","cmd.exe","mshta.exe",
                                            "rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","wscript.exe",
                                            "cscript.exe","bitsadmin.exe","certutil.exe",
                                            "curl.exe","wget.exe")
         by Processes.dest, Processes.user
     | `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
     | rename dest AS src]
| eval delta_sec = exec_time - click_time
| where delta_sec >= 0 AND delta_sec <= 60
| table click_time, exec_time, delta_sec, recipient, src, src_user, subject,
        clicked_domain, clicked_url, exec_proc, exec_cmd
| sort - click_time
Email attachment opened from external sender Delivery High

let LookbackDays = 7d;

    | where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
    | where AccountName !endswith "$"
    | project NetworkMessageId, RecipientEmailAddress,
              AttachmentFileName = FileName, AttachmentSHA256 = SHA256;
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("OUTLOOK.EXE","winword.exe","excel.exe","powerpnt.exe")

| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, FileName, ProcessCommandLine,
          InitiatingProcessFileName, AttachmentFileName, AttachmentSHA256
// Sentinel doesn't have an attachment-hash table. Best approximation:
// OfficeActivity Send/Receive activity + correlate to host process spawn.
let LookbackDays = 7d;
let MailWithAttach = OfficeActivity
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(LookbackDays)
    | where OfficeWorkload == "Exchange"
    | where Operation in~ ("Send","Receive")
    | where isnotempty(MessageId)
    | project EmailTime = TimeGenerated, MailboxOwnerUPN, Sender,
              Recipients, Subject, MessageId;

| where TimeGenerated > ago(LookbackDays)
| where EventID == 4688
| where ParentProcessName endswith "OUTLOOK.EXE"
| where NewProcessName endswith "\cmd.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\powershell.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\wscript.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\cscript.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\mshta.exe"
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account, NewProcessName, CommandLine,
          ParentProcessName
| order by TimeGenerated desc

date: 2026/05/02
tags:
    - attack.t1059.001

    category: process_creation

detection:

            - '\powershell.exe'
            - '\pwsh.exe'

            - '-enc '
            - 'EncodedCommand'
            - 'FromBase64String'
            - 'Invoke-Expression'
            - 'IEX('
            - 'DownloadString'
            - 'Net.WebClient'
            - '-w hidden'
            - '-WindowStyle Hidden'
            - '-nop'
            - '-NoProfile'
            - '-ep bypass'
            - '-ExecutionPolicy Bypass'
    condition: selection_image and selection_args
falsepositives:
    - DevOps tooling that legitimately uses encoded command for
      cross-shell escaping
level: high

File hash IOCs — endpoint file/process match Installation High
Match SHA256/SHA1/MD5 named in the article against EDR file/process telemetry.
ATT&CKT1027
Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem Endpoint.Processes DeviceFileEvents DeviceProcessEvents
// Pre-filter each table BEFORE the union — the engine cannot push
// predicates across `union`, and DeviceFileEvents lacks ProcessCommandLine
// (Initiating only) so column-projection has to differ per side.
union
    ( DeviceFileEvents
        | where Timestamp > ago(7d)
        | where InitiatingProcessAccountName !endswith "$"
        | where SHA256 in~ ("D0DB619A7A160949528D46D20FC0151BF9775C32", "99B454262DC26B081600E844371982A49D334E5E") or SHA1 in~ ("D0DB619A7A160949528D46D20FC0151BF9775C32", "99B454262DC26B081600E844371982A49D334E5E") or MD5 in~ ("D0DB619A7A160949528D46D20FC0151BF9775C32", "99B454262DC26B081600E844371982A49D334E5E")
        | project Timestamp, DeviceName, ActionType, FileName, FolderPath, SHA256,
                  Cmd = InitiatingProcessCommandLine, Source = "DeviceFileEvents" ),
    ( DeviceProcessEvents
        | where Timestamp > ago(7d)
        | where AccountName !endswith "$"
        | where SHA256 in~ ("D0DB619A7A160949528D46D20FC0151BF9775C32", "99B454262DC26B081600E844371982A49D334E5E") or SHA1 in~ ("D0DB619A7A160949528D46D20FC0151BF9775C32", "99B454262DC26B081600E844371982A49D334E5E") or MD5 in~ ("D0DB619A7A160949528D46D20FC0151BF9775C32", "99B454262DC26B081600E844371982A49D334E5E")
        | project Timestamp, DeviceName, ActionType, FileName, FolderPath, SHA256,
                  Cmd = ProcessCommandLine, Source = "DeviceProcessEvents" )
| order by Timestamp desc

// Pre-filter both ASIM tables before the union — same pattern as the
// Defender version so the engine can push hash predicates per-side.
let BadHashes = dynamic(["D0DB619A7A160949528D46D20FC0151BF9775C32", "99B454262DC26B081600E844371982A49D334E5E"]);
union
    ( ImFileEvent
        | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
        | where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
        | where TargetFileSHA256 in~ (BadHashes)
           or TargetFileSHA1 in~ (BadHashes)
           or TargetFileMD5 in~ (BadHashes)
        | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, EventType,
                  TargetFileName, TargetFilePath, TargetFileSHA256,
                  Cmd = ActingProcessName, Source = "ImFileEvent" ),
    ( ImProcessCreate
        | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
        | where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
        | where SHA256 in~ (BadHashes) or SHA1 in~ (BadHashes) or MD5 in~ (BadHashes)
        | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, EventType = TargetProcessName,
                  TargetFileName = TargetProcessName, TargetFilePath = TargetProcessName,
                  TargetFileSHA256 = SHA256,
                  Cmd = TargetProcessCommandLine, Source = "ImProcessCreate" )
| order by TimeGenerated desc

    product: windows
detection:
    selection_path:
        TargetFilename|contains:
            - '\Google\Chrome\User Data\'
            - '\Microsoft\Edge\User Data\'
            - '\Mozilla\Firefox\Profiles\'
    selection_file:
        TargetFilename|endswith:
            - '\Login Data'
            - '\Cookies'
            - '\logins.json'
            - '\cookies.sqlite'
    filter_browser:
        Image|endswith:
            - '\chrome.exe'
            - '\msedge.exe'
            - '\firefox.exe'
            - '\brave.exe'
            - '\opera.exe'
    condition: selection_path and selection_file and not filter_browser
falsepositives:
    - Backup utilities reading user profile files
    - Browser sync agents from non-stock vendors
level: high
ENDPOINT
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Filesystem
    where (Filesystem.file_path="*\Google\Chrome\User Data\*\Login Data*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Google\Chrome\User Data\*\Cookies*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Microsoft\Edge\User Data\*\Login Data*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Mozilla\Firefox\Profiles\*\logins.json*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Mozilla\Firefox\Profiles\*\cookies.sqlite*")
      AND NOT Filesystem.process_name IN ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe","brave.exe","opera.exe")
    by Filesystem.dest, Filesystem.process_name, Filesystem.file_path, Filesystem.user
| `drop_dm_object_name(Filesystem)`
| tstats summariesonly=false count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Filesystem
    where (Filesystem.file_path="*\Google\Chrome\User Data\*\Login Data*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Google\Chrome\User Data\*\Cookies*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Microsoft\Edge\User Data\*\Login Data*"

      AND NOT Filesystem.process_name IN ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe","brave.exe","opera.exe")
    by Filesystem.dest, Filesystem.process_name, Filesystem.file_path, Filesystem.user
| `drop_dm_object_name(Filesystem)`
Ransomware-style mass file rename / extension change Actions on Objectives Medium
Threshold detection: many files renamed in short window, often with new extension.
ATT&CKT1486
Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem DeviceFileEvents
DeviceFileEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where InitiatingProcessAccountName !endswith "$"
| where ActionType in ("FileRenamed","FileModified")
| summarize files = dcount(FileName) by DeviceName, InitiatingProcessAccountName, bin(Timestamp, 1m)
| where files > 200    // empirical: > 200 unique-file renames in 1m by one account on one host
                       //            is well above the P99 of legitimate bulk-tooling
| order by files desc

ImFileEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)
| where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
| where EventType in ("FileRenamed","FileModified")
| summarize files = dcount(TargetFileName)
            by DvcHostname, ActorUsername, ActingProcessName, bin(TimeGenerated, 1m)
| where files > 200    // P99 of legit bulk-tooling per-minute rename volume
| order by files desc
ENDPOINT
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count, dc(Filesystem.file_name) AS files
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Filesystem
    where Filesystem.action IN ("modified","renamed")
    by Filesystem.dest, Filesystem.user, _time span=1m
| `drop_dm_object_name(Filesystem)`
| where files > 200
| sort - files
| tstats summariesonly=false count, dc(Filesystem.file_name) AS files
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Filesystem
    where Filesystem.action IN ("modified","renamed")
    by Filesystem.dest, Filesystem.user, _time span=1m
| `drop_dm_object_name(Filesystem)`
| where files > 200
| sort - files
LSASS process access / dump (credential theft) Actions on Objectives High
Mimikatz, comsvcs.dll MiniDump, or any non-Windows process opening LSASS.
ATT&CKT1003.001 T1003
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes DeviceEvents
DeviceEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where AccountName !endswith "$"
| where ActionType == "OpenProcessApiCall"
| where FileName =~ "lsass.exe"
| where InitiatingProcessFileName !in~ ("MsSense.exe","MsMpEng.exe","csrss.exe",
                                          "svchost.exe","wininit.exe","services.exe",
                                          "lsm.exe","SearchProtocolHost.exe")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, ActionType, FileName,
          InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine,
          InitiatingProcessFolderPath, AccountName
| order by Timestamp desc
// SecurityEvent EventID 4663 = object access. AccessMask 0x10 / 0x40 / 0x1010
// indicate read access to lsass.exe — credential-dump signature. Audit
// Object Access > Audit File System policy required.
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where EventID == 4663
| where ObjectName endswith "\lsass.exe"
| where Account !endswith "$"
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account, ObjectName, ProcessName,
          AccessMask, AccessReason
| order by TimeGenerated desc

title: LSASS Process Access Outside Microsoft-Signed Tooling
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d04
status: experimental
description: |
    Anything opening lsass.exe with read-memory access that isn't a
    Microsoft signed component (Defender, csrss, etc.) is suspect.
    Mimikatz / Pypykatz / nanodump signature.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1003/001/
author: Clankerusecase
date: 2026/05/02
tags:
    - attack.t1003.001
    - attack.credential-access
logsource:
    category: process_access
    product: windows
detection:
    selection:
        TargetImage|endswith: '\lsass.exe'
    filter_known_good:
        SourceImage|endswith:
            - '\MsSense.exe'
            - '\MsMpEng.exe'
            - '\csrss.exe'
            - '\svchost.exe'
            - '\wininit.exe'
            - '\services.exe'
            - '\lsm.exe'
            - '\SearchProtocolHost.exe'
    condition: selection and not filter_known_good
falsepositives:
    - Third-party EDR / forensic tools — extend the filter list
level: high
ENDPOINT
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.

        OR Processes.process="*MiniDump*" OR Processes.process="*comsvcs.dll*MiniDump*"
        OR Processes.process="*procdump*lsass*")
       OR (Processes.process_name="rundll32.exe" AND Processes.process="*comsvcs*MiniDump*")
    by Processes.dest, Processes.user, Processes.process_name, Processes.process, Processes.parent_process_name
| `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`

    from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
    where (Processes.process="*lsass*" OR Processes.process="*sekurlsa*"
        OR Processes.process="*MiniDump*" OR Processes.process="*comsvcs.dll*MiniDump*"

    by Processes.dest, Processes.user, Processes.process_name, Processes.process, Processes.parent_process_name
| `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
Remote service execution — PsExec / SMB lateral movement Actions on Objectives High
psexec / paexec / smbexec / wmic /node — service install over SMB from remote host.
ATT&CKT1021.002 T1569.002
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes DeviceProcessEvents
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where AccountName !endswith "$"
| where FileName in~ ("psexec.exe","psexesvc.exe","paexec.exe","smbexec.py")
   or (FileName =~ "wmic.exe" and ProcessCommandLine has "/node:")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, FileName, ProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessFileName
| order by Timestamp desc

SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where EventID == 4688            // process-create (audit-policy required)
| where Account !endswith "$"
| where NewProcessName endswith "\psexec.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\psexesvc.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\paexec.exe"
   or (NewProcessName endswith "\wmic.exe" and CommandLine has "/node:")
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account, NewProcessName, CommandLine,
          ParentProcessName
| order by TimeGenerated desc

Trusted vendor binary / installer launching unusual children Exploitation Medium
Supply-chain trojan signal: legitimate signed binary spawning script interpreters or exotic LOLBins.
ATT&CKT1195.002
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes DeviceProcessEvents
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where AccountName !endswith "$"
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe")
| where FileName in~ ("powershell.exe","cmd.exe","rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","mshta.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe","wmic.exe","bitsadmin.exe")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, InitiatingProcessFileName, FileName, ProcessCommandLine
// Replace `"setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe"` with the vendor binaries observed in the
// article (matching shape with the Defender query).
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where EventID == 4688
| where Account !endswith "$"
| where ParentProcessName has_any ("setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe")
| where NewProcessName endswith "\powershell.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\cmd.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\rundll32.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\regsvr32.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\mshta.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\wscript.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\cscript.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\wmic.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\bitsadmin.exe"
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account, ParentProcessName,
          NewProcessName, CommandLine
| order by TimeGenerated desc

title: Trusted Vendor Binary Launching Unusual Children
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d09
status: experimental
description: |
    A signed vendor binary (in the article's named-vendors list)
    spawning script-host or LOLBin children. Strongest signal when
    paired with a recent CVE / supply-chain advisory naming the same
    vendor.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1195/
author: Clankerusecase
date: 2026/05/02
tags:
    - attack.t1195
    - attack.initial-access
logsource:
    category: process_creation
    product: windows
detection:
    selection_child:
        Image|endswith:
            - '\powershell.exe'
            - '\pwsh.exe'
            - '\cmd.exe'
            - '\rundll32.exe'
            - '\regsvr32.exe'
            - '\mshta.exe'
            - '\wscript.exe'
            - '\cscript.exe'
            - '\wmic.exe'
            - '\bitsadmin.exe'
    selection_parent_vendor:
        ParentImage|contains:
            - '\Program Files\'
            - '\Program Files (x86)\'
    condition: selection_child and selection_parent_vendor
falsepositives:
    - Many — installers and updaters legitimately spawn children. Pin
      the parent path to specific vendor binaries observed in the
      article before alerting.
level: medium
ENDPOINT
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
    where Processes.parent_process_name IN ("setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe")
      AND Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","cmd.exe","rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","mshta.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe","wmic.exe","bitsadmin.exe")
    by Processes.dest, Processes.user, Processes.parent_process_name, Processes.process_name, Processes.process
| `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
| tstats summariesonly=false count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
    where Processes.parent_process_name IN ("setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe")
      AND Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","cmd.exe","rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","mshta.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe","wmic.exe","bitsadmin.exe")
    by Processes.dest, Processes.user, Processes.parent_process_name, Processes.process_name, Processes.process
| `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`

// TVM tables are daily snapshots — pin a 1-day window so the engine
// partitions on Timestamp instead of scanning the full retention.
DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where CveId in~ ("CVE-2017-7921")
| join kind=inner (DeviceInfo | where Timestamp > ago(1d)) on DeviceId
| project DeviceName, OSPlatform, CveId, VulnerabilitySeverityLevel, RecommendedSecurityUpdate
| order by VulnerabilitySeverityLevel asc, DeviceName asc

// Sentinel has no native TVM table. If MDE→Sentinel streaming is enabled,
// `DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities` is available. Otherwise this UC has
// no Sentinel translation — track via Update / Heartbeat OS-version
// gaps as a coarse approximation.

| where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)
| where CveId in~ ("CVE-2017-7921")
| join kind=inner (DeviceInfo | where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)) on DeviceId
| project DeviceName, OSPlatform, CveId,
          VulnerabilitySeverityLevel, RecommendedSecurityUpdate
| order by VulnerabilitySeverityLevel asc, DeviceName asc

CISA KEV: CVE-2021-22681 — Rockwell Multiple Products Insufficient Protected Credentials Vulnerability

CISA KEV
2026-03-05 1 use case 0 techniques 3 kill-chain phases detected

Multiple Rockwell products contain an insufficient protected credentials vulnerability. Studio 5000 Logix Designer software may allow a key to be discovered. This key is used to verify Logix controllers are communicating with Rockwell Automation design software. Weaponization. Coupling exploit code with deliverable payload (e.g. weaponized PDF/Office doc, trojanized installer).
Delivery. Transmitting the weapon (phishing email, USB, watering hole, drive-by, malicious ad).
Exploitation. Triggering the exploit — vulnerability/macro/trust abuse to execute code.
Installation. Establishing persistence on the host — backdoor, service, scheduled task, registry key.
Command & Control. Beacon to attacker infrastructure for control and tasking.
Actions on Objectives. Lateral movement, credential dumping, data theft, ransomware, sabotage.
Asset exposure — vulnerability matches article CVE(s) Reconnaissance High
Match vulnerability scanner output to the CVE(s) named in the article.
ATT&CKT1190
Data sourcesVulnerabilities DeviceInfo DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
// TVM tables are daily snapshots — pin a 1-day window so the engine
// partitions on Timestamp instead of scanning the full retention.
DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where CveId in~ ("CVE-2021-30952")
| join kind=inner (DeviceInfo | where Timestamp > ago(1d)) on DeviceId
| project DeviceName, OSPlatform, CveId, VulnerabilitySeverityLevel, RecommendedSecurityUpdate
| order by VulnerabilitySeverityLevel asc, DeviceName asc

// Sentinel has no native TVM table. If MDE→Sentinel streaming is enabled,
// `DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities` is available. Otherwise this UC has
// no Sentinel translation — track via Update / Heartbeat OS-version
// gaps as a coarse approximation.
DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
| where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)
| where CveId in~ ("CVE-2021-30952")
| join kind=inner (DeviceInfo | where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)) on DeviceId
| project DeviceName, OSPlatform, CveId,
          VulnerabilitySeverityLevel, RecommendedSecurityUpdate
| order by VulnerabilitySeverityLevel asc, DeviceName asc

Infostealer — non-browser process accessing browser cookie/login DBs Actions on Objectives High
Stealers (RedLine, Lumma, Vidar, Atomic) read Login Data / cookies SQLite from Chrome/Edge/Firefox.
ATT&CKT1539 T1555.003
Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem DeviceFileEvents

| where Timestamp > ago(7d)

| where FolderPath has_any (@"\Google\Chrome\User Data\", @"\Microsoft\Edge\User Data\", @"\Mozilla\Firefox\Profiles\")
| where FileName in~ ("Login Data","Cookies","logins.json","cookies.sqlite")

title: Infostealer - Non-Browser Process Accessing Browser Credential Stores
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d01
status: experimental
description: |
    Stealers (RedLine, Lumma, Vidar, Atomic) read Login Data / cookies
    SQLite databases from Chrome/Edge/Firefox profile directories. Any
    non-browser process touching these files is a high-fidelity signal.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1539/
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1555/003/
author: Clankerusecase
date: 2026/05/02
tags:
    - attack.t1539
    - attack.t1555.003
    - attack.credential-access
logsource:
    category: file_access
    product: windows
detection:
    selection_path:
        TargetFilename|contains:
            - '\Google\Chrome\User Data\'
            - '\Microsoft\Edge\User Data\'
            - '\Mozilla\Firefox\Profiles\'
    selection_file:
        TargetFilename|endswith:
            - '\Login Data'
            - '\Cookies'
            - '\logins.json'
            - '\cookies.sqlite'
    filter_browser:
        Image|endswith:
            - '\chrome.exe'
            - '\msedge.exe'
            - '\firefox.exe'
            - '\brave.exe'
            - '\opera.exe'
    condition: selection_path and selection_file and not filter_browser
falsepositives:
    - Backup utilities reading user profile files
    - Browser sync agents from non-stock vendors
level: high

    from datamodel=Endpoint.Filesystem
    where (Filesystem.file_path="*\Google\Chrome\User Data\*\Login Data*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Google\Chrome\User Data\*\Cookies*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Microsoft\Edge\User Data\*\Login Data*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Mozilla\Firefox\Profiles\*\logins.json*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Mozilla\Firefox\Profiles\*\cookies.sqlite*")
      AND NOT Filesystem.process_name IN ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe","brave.exe","opera.exe")
    by Filesystem.dest, Filesystem.process_name, Filesystem.file_path, Filesystem.user
| `drop_dm_object_name(Filesystem)`

Phishing-link click correlated to endpoint execution Delivery High
User received an external email containing a URL, clicked it, and within 60 seconds the recipient's endpoint spawned a scripting interpreter or network downloader (PowerShell / cmd / mshta / curl / certutil / bitsadmin). This is the high-fidelity shape — a raw "user clicked an emailed URL" query is too noisy to alert on because every Microsoft 365 tenant generates thousands of legitimate clicks per day. The exec-correlation step suppresses the noise to roughly the rate at which a click actually drives onto-host execution, which is the actionable signal. Pair with UC_PHISH_LINK_BYPASS (Safe Links override) and UC_PHISH_LINK_NEWDOMAIN for orthogonal high-fidelity variants.
ATT&CKT1566.002 T1204.001 T1059.001
Data sourcesEmail.All_Email Web Endpoint.Processes EmailEvents EmailUrlInfo UrlClickEvents DeviceProcessEvents
// Phishing-link click that drives endpoint execution within 60s.
// Far higher fidelity than "every clicked URL" — most legitimate clicks
// never spawn a non-browser child process, so the join eliminates the
// 99% of noise that makes a raw click query unactionable.
let LookbackDays = 7d;
let SuspectClicks = UrlClickEvents
    | where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
    | where AccountName !endswith "$"
    | where ActionType in ("ClickAllowed","ClickedThrough")
    | join kind=inner (
        EmailEvents
        | where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
        | where DeliveryAction == "Delivered"
        | where EmailDirection == "Inbound"
        | project NetworkMessageId, Subject, SenderFromAddress, SenderFromDomain,
                  RecipientEmailAddress, EmailTimestamp = Timestamp
      ) on NetworkMessageId
    | join kind=leftouter (
        EmailUrlInfo | project NetworkMessageId, Url, UrlDomain
      ) on NetworkMessageId, Url
    | project ClickTime = Timestamp, AccountUpn, IPAddress, Url, UrlDomain,
              Subject, SenderFromAddress, SenderFromDomain, RecipientEmailAddress,
              ActionType;
// Correlate to a non-browser child process spawned within 60 seconds on
// the recipient's device.
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe",
                                         "outlook.exe","brave.exe","arc.exe")
| where FileName in~ ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","cmd.exe","mshta.exe",
                        "rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe",
                        "bitsadmin.exe","certutil.exe","curl.exe","wget.exe")
| join kind=inner SuspectClicks on $left.AccountName == $right.AccountUpn
| where Timestamp between (ClickTime .. ClickTime + 60s)
| project ClickTime, ProcessTime = Timestamp,
          DelaySec = datetime_diff('second', Timestamp, ClickTime),
          DeviceName, AccountName, RecipientEmailAddress, SenderFromAddress,
          Subject, Url, UrlDomain, ActionType,
          FileName, ProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessFileName
| order by ClickTime desc
// UrlClickEvents has no clean Sentinel-native equivalent. The detection
// pivots to: Office Send→Receive proximity to SecurityEvent 4688 of a
// non-browser child process. Lower fidelity than the Defender version.
let LookbackDays = 7d;
let SuspectMailUsers = OfficeActivity
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(LookbackDays)
    | where OfficeWorkload == "Exchange"
    | where Operation =~ "Receive"
    | summarize Subject = any(Subject), Sender = any(Sender)
                by MailboxOwnerUPN, MessageId, EmailTime = bin(TimeGenerated, 1m);
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(LookbackDays)
| where EventID == 4688
| where ParentProcessName has_any ("\chrome.exe","\msedge.exe",
                                     "\firefox.exe","\outlook.exe","\brave.exe")
| where NewProcessName has_any ("\powershell.exe","\pwsh.exe","\cmd.exe",
                                  "\mshta.exe","\rundll32.exe","\regsvr32.exe",
                                  "\wscript.exe","\cscript.exe","\bitsadmin.exe",
                                  "\certutil.exe","\curl.exe","\wget.exe")
| join kind=inner SuspectMailUsers on $left.Account == $right.MailboxOwnerUPN
| where TimeGenerated between (EmailTime .. EmailTime + 60s)
| project TimeGenerated, EmailTime, Computer, Account, ParentProcessName,
          NewProcessName, CommandLine, Sender, Subject
| order by TimeGenerated desc
WEB
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
``` Phishing-link click that drives endpoint execution within 60s ```
| tstats `summariesonly` earliest(_time) AS click_time
    from datamodel=Web
    where Web.action="allowed"
    by Web.src, Web.user, Web.dest, Web.url
| `drop_dm_object_name(Web)`
| rename user AS recipient, dest AS clicked_domain, url AS clicked_url
| join type=inner recipient
    [| tstats `summariesonly` count
         from datamodel=Email.All_Email
         where All_Email.action="delivered" AND All_Email.url!="-"
         by All_Email.recipient, All_Email.src_user, All_Email.url, All_Email.subject
     | `drop_dm_object_name(All_Email)`
     | rex field=url "https?://(?<email_domain>[^/]+)"
     | rename recipient AS recipient]
| join type=inner src
    [| tstats `summariesonly` earliest(_time) AS exec_time
         values(Processes.process) AS exec_cmd, values(Processes.process_name) AS exec_proc
         from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
         where Processes.parent_process_name IN ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe",
                                                   "outlook.exe","brave.exe","arc.exe")
           AND Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","cmd.exe","mshta.exe",
                                            "rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","wscript.exe",
                                            "cscript.exe","bitsadmin.exe","certutil.exe",
                                            "curl.exe","wget.exe")
         by Processes.dest, Processes.user
     | `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
     | rename dest AS src]
| eval delta_sec = exec_time - click_time
| where delta_sec >= 0 AND delta_sec <= 60
| table click_time, exec_time, delta_sec, recipient, src, src_user, subject,
        clicked_domain, clicked_url, exec_proc, exec_cmd
| sort - click_time

                                            "rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","wscript.exe",
                                            "cscript.exe","bitsadmin.exe","certutil.exe",
                                            "curl.exe","wget.exe")

Email attachment opened from external sender Delivery High

Data sourcesEmail.All_Email Endpoint.Processes EmailAttachmentInfo DeviceProcessEvents
let LookbackDays = 7d;
let MalAttachments = EmailAttachmentInfo
    | where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
    | where AccountName !endswith "$"
    | project NetworkMessageId, RecipientEmailAddress,
              AttachmentFileName = FileName, AttachmentSHA256 = SHA256;
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("OUTLOOK.EXE","winword.exe","excel.exe","powerpnt.exe")
| where FileName in~ ("cmd.exe","powershell.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe",

| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, FileName, ProcessCommandLine,
          InitiatingProcessFileName, AttachmentFileName, AttachmentSHA256
// Sentinel doesn't have an attachment-hash table. Best approximation:
// OfficeActivity Send/Receive activity + correlate to host process spawn.
let LookbackDays = 7d;
let MailWithAttach = OfficeActivity
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(LookbackDays)
    | where OfficeWorkload == "Exchange"
    | where Operation in~ ("Send","Receive")
    | where isnotempty(MessageId)
    | project EmailTime = TimeGenerated, MailboxOwnerUPN, Sender,
              Recipients, Subject, MessageId;
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(LookbackDays)
| where EventID == 4688
| where ParentProcessName endswith "OUTLOOK.EXE"
| where NewProcessName endswith "\cmd.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\powershell.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\wscript.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\cscript.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\mshta.exe"
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account, NewProcessName, CommandLine,
          ParentProcessName
| order by TimeGenerated desc

title: PsExec / SMB Lateral Movement Tooling Execution
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d03
status: experimental
description: |
    PsExec, paexec, smbexec, csexec, remcom — admin-grade tooling that
    pivots over SMB. Pair with `wmic /node:` for the full cluster.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1021/002/
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1569/002/
author: Clankerusecase

Ransomware-style mass file rename / extension change Actions on Objectives Medium
Threshold detection: many files renamed in short window, often with new extension.
ATT&CKT1486
Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem DeviceFileEvents
DeviceFileEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where InitiatingProcessAccountName !endswith "$"
| where ActionType in ("FileRenamed","FileModified")
| summarize files = dcount(FileName) by DeviceName, InitiatingProcessAccountName, bin(Timestamp, 1m)
| where files > 200    // empirical: > 200 unique-file renames in 1m by one account on one host
                       //            is well above the P99 of legitimate bulk-tooling
| order by files desc

ImFileEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)
| where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
| where EventType in ("FileRenamed","FileModified")
| summarize files = dcount(TargetFileName)
            by DvcHostname, ActorUsername, ActingProcessName, bin(TimeGenerated, 1m)
| where files > 200    // P99 of legit bulk-tooling per-minute rename volume
| order by files desc
ENDPOINT
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count, dc(Filesystem.file_name) AS files
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Filesystem
    where Filesystem.action IN ("modified","renamed")
    by Filesystem.dest, Filesystem.user, _time span=1m
| `drop_dm_object_name(Filesystem)`
| where files > 200
| sort - files
| tstats summariesonly=false count, dc(Filesystem.file_name) AS files
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Filesystem
    where Filesystem.action IN ("modified","renamed")
    by Filesystem.dest, Filesystem.user, _time span=1m
| `drop_dm_object_name(Filesystem)`
| where files > 200
| sort - files
LSASS process access / dump (credential theft) Actions on Objectives High
Mimikatz, comsvcs.dll MiniDump, or any non-Windows process opening LSASS.
ATT&CKT1003.001 T1003
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes DeviceEvents
DeviceEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where AccountName !endswith "$"
| where ActionType == "OpenProcessApiCall"
| where FileName =~ "lsass.exe"
| where InitiatingProcessFileName !in~ ("MsSense.exe","MsMpEng.exe","csrss.exe",
                                          "svchost.exe","wininit.exe","services.exe",
                                          "lsm.exe","SearchProtocolHost.exe")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, ActionType, FileName,
          InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine,
          InitiatingProcessFolderPath, AccountName
| order by Timestamp desc
// SecurityEvent EventID 4663 = object access. AccessMask 0x10 / 0x40 / 0x1010
// indicate read access to lsass.exe — credential-dump signature. Audit
// Object Access > Audit File System policy required.
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where EventID == 4663
| where ObjectName endswith "\lsass.exe"
| where Account !endswith "$"
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account, ObjectName, ProcessName,
          AccessMask, AccessReason
| order by TimeGenerated desc
